# Method 2 — Silent installation using a response file
Supply configuration values in a .rsp file and run runInstaller -silent.
- No graphical interface required.
- Repeatable and suitable for automation.
- Excellent DBA practice for building multiple lab.

### Note : Oracle's official documentation states that Oracle Database 19c 19.32 or later is recommended on Oracle Linux 9. It describes downloading the 19.3 Database Home image and applying the appropriate Release Update during installation.

### First confirm OS version and architecture so we can select the correct 19c installation route.
```
cat /etc/os-release
uname -r
uname -m

Output :
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$ cat /etc/os-release
NAME="Oracle Linux Server"
VERSION="9.8"
ID="ol"
ID_LIKE="fedora"
VARIANT="Server"
VARIANT_ID="server"
VERSION_ID="9.8"
PLATFORM_ID="platform:el9"
PRETTY_NAME="Oracle Linux Server 9.8"
ANSI_COLOR="0;31"
CPE_NAME="cpe:/o:oracle:linux:9:8:server"
HOME_URL="https://linux.oracle.com/"
BUG_REPORT_URL="https://github.com/oracle/oracle-linux"

ORACLE_BUGZILLA_PRODUCT="Oracle Linux 9"
ORACLE_BUGZILLA_PRODUCT_VERSION=9.8
ORACLE_SUPPORT_PRODUCT="Oracle Linux"
ORACLE_SUPPORT_PRODUCT_VERSION=9.8
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$ uname -r
6.12.0-203.76.7.3.el9uek.x86_64
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$ uname -m
x86_64
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$
```

## Step 1 — Inventory your existing software and patches
```
[svanapal@orion9 DB_Patch]$ ls -lh /media/sf_Sunil-Tech-HyperLab/ISO/Oracle-Softwares/Oracle_Patches/19300_Patches/DB_Software/V982063-01_Db.zip
-rwxrwx---. 1 root vboxsf 2.9G Jan 23  2024 /media/sf_Sunil-Tech-HyperLab/ISO/Oracle-Softwares/Oracle_Patches/19300_Patches/DB_Software/V982063-01_Db.zip
```

## Step 2 — Verify the Oracle installation user and home
```
We'll use the Oracle Home path from our lab setup:
/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
Run the following commands as svanapal:

echo "===== CURRENT USER ====="
id

echo
echo "===== ORACLE HOME PARENT ====="
ls -ld /u01 /u01/app /u01/app/oracle 2>&1

echo
echo "===== DATABASE HOME ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1 2>&1

echo
echo "===== BASE ZIP ====="
ls -lh /media/sf_Sunil-Tech-HyperLab/ISO/Oracle-Softwares/Oracle_Patches/19300_Patches/DB_Software/V982063-01_Db.zip

The Oracle Home directory has not been created yet, so we need to establish the correct ownership and directory structure before extraction.
```
## Step 3 - Create the Oracle groups
We'll create the Oracle Home as root, then give ownership to the Oracle software owner, svanapal.
First, verify whether the required Oracle groups already exist. Run this command as your current user:
```
echo "===== ORACLE USER ====="
getent passwd svanapal

echo
echo "===== ORACLE GROUPS ====="
getent group oinstall
getent group dba
getent group oper

echo
echo "===== U01 FILESYSTEM ====="
df -hT /u01
```
We found two things to address before installing Oracle 19c:
- The groups oinstall and dba do not exist.
- /u01 has a 20 GB filesystem with approximately 20 GB free.
  That may be tight once the database software and patches are installed, so we'll monitor space carefully.

```
Create the Oracle groups. Run the following commands using sudo:
sudo groupadd oinstall
sudo groupadd dba

sudo usermod -g oinstall -aG dba svanapal

Verify the groups. Run these commands as svanapal:
echo "===== USER GROUP MEMBERSHIP ====="
id svanapal

echo
echo "===== OINSTALL GROUP ====="
getent group oinstall

echo
echo "===== DBA GROUP ====="
getent group dba

=============================================================================================
OUTPUT:
===== USER GROUP MEMBERSHIP =====
uid=1000(svanapal) gid=1001(oinstall) groups=1001(oinstall),10(wheel),995(vboxsf),1002(dba)

===== OINSTALL GROUP =====
oinstall:x:1001:

===== DBA GROUP =====
dba:x:1002:svanapal
=============================================================================================
```

## Step 4 — Create the Oracle Home directory

Create the Oracle directories
We will create the Oracle Base, Database Home, and central inventory directories. 
The inventory will be outside the Database Home so it can track Oracle installations independently.
Run the following commands:
```
sudo mkdir -p /u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
sudo mkdir -p /u01/app/oraInventory

sudo chown -R svanapal:oinstall /u01/app/oracle
sudo chown -R svanapal:oinstall /u01/app/oraInventory

sudo chmod -R 775 /u01/app/oracle
sudo chmod -R 775 /u01/app/oraInventory

Verify the Results:
echo "===== ORACLE BASE ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oracle

echo
echo "===== DATABASE HOME ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1

echo
echo "===== CENTRAL INVENTORY ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oraInventory

echo
echo "===== AVAILABLE SPACE ====="
df -h /u01
```

We will extract the base software directly into the Oracle Home. Verify that unzip is available.

```
-d /u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
```

Several actionable findings. Lets do the OS readiness

## OS and architecture identified
Oracle Linux Server 9.8, UEK kernel, x86_64. These identify the platform we need to validate against Oracle's OL9-specific installation guidance.

## Memory and swap
7.7 GiB RAM and 2 GiB swap are available. Current memory availability is good, but the installer and linking process may need additional resources.

## Four packages missing from our initial screening
bc, ksh, libaio-devel and sysstat are not installed. We'll check Oracle's applicable prerequisites before deciding which packages to add.

## Open-file limit
Your current shell has ulimit -n set to 1024. We need to examine the Oracle user's configured limits and Oracle's requirements before proceeding.

## Storage
/u01 has about 14 GiB free after extraction. That is a constraint worth monitoring during installation. We should also verify the intended locations for database files and logs.

## Decide how to configure the prerequisites
Oracle documents two approaches for OL9:
1. Install oracle-database-preinstall-19c, which installs required packages and configures several OS prerequisites.
2. Configure prerequisites manually, installing the required packages and applying the necessary kernel and resource-limit settings yourself.

Recommend checking the preinstallation RPM first. 
Since we already created the custom svanapal user and Oracle groups, we should verify what the RPM would configure rather than blindly changing the user setup.

```
echo "===== PREINSTALL RPM STATUS ====="
rpm -q oracle-database-preinstall-19c  // package oracle-database-preinstall-19c is not installed

echo
echo "===== ENABLED REPOSITORIES ====="
sudo dnf repolist --enabled

echo
echo "===== PREINSTALL RPM CANDIDATE ====="
dnf info oracle-database-preinstall-19c

echo
echo "===== HOSTNAME ====="
hostname
hostname -f
```

# Decision: use the manual prerequisite route for this installation
Your oracle-database-preinstall-19c package is available as version 1.0-3.el9 for x86_64. It installs required dependencies and configures operating-system prerequisites. 
Oracle's documentation also states that it creates a standard oracle user and the oinstall and dba groups. 
Because we have already configured svanapal as the Oracle software owner, I recommend keeping that account and configuring the prerequisites explicitly for this learning exercise. 
This avoids introducing an additional Oracle owner account unnecessarily.

### Install the missing packages
Run the following command to install the four packages
```
sudo dnf install -y bc ksh libaio-devel sysstat
rpm -q bc ksh libaio-devel sysstat
```
### Check the complete OL9 package list
```
for p in \
  bc binutils compat-openssl11 elfutils-libelf fontconfig \
  glibc glibc-devel ksh libaio libX11 libXau libXi \

  libnsl libstdc++ libxcb libvirt-libs make \
  policycoreutils policycoreutils-python-utils smartmontools \

do
  rpm -q "$p" >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    && printf "INSTALLED  %s\n" "$p" \
    || printf "MISSING    %s\n" "$p"
done
```

### Install the remaining candidate packages 
```
sudo dnf install \
  compat-openssl11 fontconfig \
  libX11 libXau libXi libXrender libXtst \
  libibverbs libnsl libxcb libvirt-libs \
  smartmontools xz-devel
```

# Oracle Linux 9.8 Kernel Parameter Assessment

## Kernel Parameter Assessment

The following table compares the current kernel settings with the documented Oracle Database 19c Linux installation minimums used for this assessment.

| Parameter | Current Value | Documented Minimum | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `kernel.shmmni` | 4096 | 4096 | Meets |
| `kernel.sem` — `semmsl` | 32000 | 250 | Meets |
| `kernel.sem` — `semmns` | 1024000000 | 32000 | Meets |
| `kernel.sem` — `semopm` | 500 | 100 | Meets |
| `kernel.sem` — `semmni` | 32000 | 128 | Meets |
| `fs.file-max` | 9223372036854775807 | 6815744 | Exceeds |
| `net.core.rmem_default` | 229376 | 262144 | Below |
| `net.core.rmem_max` | 229376 | 4194304 | Below |
| `net.core.wmem_default` | 229376 | 262144 | Below |
| `net.core.wmem_max` | 229376 | 1048576 | Below |

**Important:** Confirm these values against the Oracle installation guide applicable to the selected Release Update and operating-system configuration before making changes.

# Audit the remaining kernel and user settings. 
We still need to check asynchronous I/O, the local TCP port range, reverse-path filtering, panic_on_oops, user resource limits and Transparent HugePages.

```
echo "===== ASYNCHRONOUS I/O ====="
sysctl fs.aio-max-nr

echo
echo "===== LOCAL PORT RANGE ====="
sysctl net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range

echo
echo "===== REVERSE-PATH FILTERING ====="
sysctl net.ipv4.conf.all.rp_filter
sysctl net.ipv4.conf.default.rp_filter

echo
echo "===== KERNEL PANIC ON OOPS ====="
sysctl kernel.panic_on_oops

echo
echo "===== USER LIMIT CONFIGURATION ====="
grep -R -nE 'svanapal|oracle|nofile|nproc|stack|memlock' \
  /etc/security/limits.conf /etc/security/limits.d 2>/dev/null

echo
echo "===== PAM LIMITS MODULE ====="
grep -R -n pam_limits.so /etc/pam.d 2>/dev/null

echo
echo "===== TRANSPARENT HUGE PAGES ====="
cat /sys/kernel/mm/transparent_hugepage/enabled
cat /sys/kernel/mm/transparent_hugepage/defrag
```
***Important: *** 
Oracle recommends disabling Transparent HugePages and using standard HugePages for enhanced database performance.

## Kernel Parameters, Network Settings, PAM Limits, and Transparent HugePages

### Initial Assessment

The following settings were reviewed on the Oracle Linux server as part of the Oracle Database 19c installation readiness assessment.

| Setting | Current Value | Initial Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| `fs.aio-max-nr` | `65536` | Below the commonly documented Oracle Database 19c Linux value of `1048576`. Verify the requirement for the applicable Oracle Database release and installation platform. |
| Local port range | `32768 60999` | Record and compare with the applicable Oracle installation requirements. |
| `net.ipv4.conf.all.rp_filter` | `0` | Reverse path filtering is disabled for all interfaces. Verify the intended configuration against the applicable OS and Oracle guidance. |
| `net.ipv4.conf.default.rp_filter` | `1` | Reverse path filtering is enabled by default for newly created interfaces. Review alongside the `all` and per-interface settings. |
| `kernel.panic_on_oops` | `1` | Review against the applicable Oracle Linux and Oracle Database configuration guidance. |
| PAM limits | `pam_limits.so` present in several PAM configuration files | Positive indication that PAM limit enforcement is configured. Verify the effective limits for the Oracle software owner and relevant processes. |
| Transparent HugePages (THP) | `[always]` | Transparent HugePages is enabled, with `always` selected as the active mode. Oracle recommends disabling Transparent HugePages for applicable database deployments. |

### Key Findings

The following items require further investigation before proceeding with the Oracle Database 19c installation:

1. **Asynchronous I/O (`fs.aio-max-nr`)** — The current value is `65536`. Compare it with Oracle's documented requirements and determine whether an adjustment is required.
2. **Transparent HugePages** — The current mode is `[always]`. Verify the applicable Oracle recommendation and plan the required configuration change if necessary.
3. **PAM limits** — Confirm the effective `nofile`, `nproc`, and other applicable resource limits for the Oracle software owner.
4. **Network and kernel parameters** — Validate the local port range, reverse path filtering, and `kernel.panic_on_oops` settings against the relevant Oracle Database 19c and Oracle Linux documentation.

# Check the Oracle user's actual resource limits

## Step 18 — Resource Limits and Hostname Validation

### 18.1 Resource Limits Assessment

The following table compares the actual resource limits of the Oracle software owner with the Oracle Database 19c installation guidelines.

| Setting | Current Value | Oracle Guideline | Result |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Soft open files (`nofile`) | `1024` | At least `1024` | Meets |
| Hard open files (`nofile`) | `524288` | At least `65536` | Meets |
| Soft processes (`nproc`) | `31336` | At least `2047` | Meets |
| Hard processes (`nproc`) | `31336` | At least `16384` | Meets |
| Soft stack (`stack`) | `8192 KB` | At least `10240 KB` | Below requirement |
| Hard stack (`stack`) | `Unlimited` | At least `10240 KB` and at most `32768 KB` | Review required |
| Soft locked memory (`memlock`) | `8192 KB` | Depends on HugePages configuration | Review required |
| Hard locked memory (`memlock`) | `8192 KB` | Depends on HugePages configuration | Review required |

**Reference:** [Oracle Database 19c — Checking Resource Limits for Oracle Software Installation Users](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/19/ladbi/checking-resource-limits-for-oracle-software-installation-users.html)

### 18.2 Assessment Findings

The following resource limits require further investigation:

1. **Soft stack limit:** The current value is `8192 KB`, which is below the documented minimum of `10240 KB`.
2. **Hard stack limit:** The current value is `Unlimited`. Review and configure it according to the applicable Oracle Database 19c installation requirements.
3. **Locked memory limits:** Both soft and hard limits are `8192 KB`. Verify the required values based on the planned HugePages configuration and memory-management strategy.

The open-file and process limits meet the documented minimum values shown above.

```
echo "===== SYSCTL CONFIGURATION FILES ====="

/etc/sysctl.conf /etc/sysctl.d 2>/dev/null

```

Now check result
Good. The search returned no matching entries in /etc/sysctl.conf or /etc/sysctl.d. That means we haven't found explicit persistent settings for these parameters in those files; the values may still come from kernel defaults or other configuration mechanisms.

Run this read-only command as svanapal:

```
echo "===== ASYNC I/O ====="

sysctl net.core.wmem_max
```

## Kernel Parameters — Oracle Database 19c

### Configuration Values

The following kernel parameters are configured for Oracle Database 19c installation readiness assessment.

| Parameter | Configured Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `fs.aio-max-nr` | `1048576` | Maximum number of asynchronous I/O requests allowed across the system |
| `kernel.panic_on_oops` | `1` | Controls kernel panic behavior when an oops or BUG condition occurs |
| `net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range` | `9000 65500` | Defines the range of local ephemeral ports |
| `net.core.rmem_default` | `262144` | Default receive socket buffer size in bytes |
| `net.core.rmem_max` | `4194304` | Maximum receive socket buffer size in bytes |
| `net.core.wmem_default` | `262144` | Default send socket buffer size in bytes |
| `net.core.wmem_max` | `1048576` | Maximum send socket buffer size in bytes |
| `net.ipv4.conf.all.rp_filter` | `0` | Disables reverse path filtering for the aggregate `all` setting |
| `net.ipv4.conf.default.rp_filter` | `1` | Sets reverse path filtering to enabled for the default interface configuration |

### Configuration File

These parameters can be maintained in a dedicated file under `/etc/sysctl.d/`, for example:

`/etc/sysctl.d/99-oracle-database.conf`

```ini
fs.aio-max-nr = 1048576
kernel.panic_on_oops = 1
net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range = 9000 65500
net.core.rmem_default = 262144
net.core.rmem_max = 4194304
net.core.wmem_default = 262144
net.core.wmem_max = 1048576
net.ipv4.conf.all.rp_filter = 0
net.ipv4.conf.default.rp_filter = 1
```

### Prepare the persistent sysctl configuration

```bash
sudo tee /etc/sysctl.d/98-oracle19c.conf > /dev/null <<'EOF'
fs.aio-max-nr = 1048576
kernel.panic_on_oops = 1
net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range = 9000 65500
net.core.rmem_default = 262144
net.core.rmem_max = 4194304
net.core.wmem_default = 262144
net.core.wmem_max = 1048576
EOF
```

### Configure Transparent HugePages persistently

Your host uses UEK 8, and the current Transparent HugePages (THP) mode is always. Oracle's guidance for UEK 7 and later recommends madvise rather than always.
Before changing the boot configuration, let's inspect the GRUB configuration and identify which bootloader configuration method is available on your VM.

```bash
echo "===== GRUB CONFIGURATION ====="
sudo grep -nE 'GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX|GRUB_ENABLE_BLSCFG' \
  /etc/default/grub

echo
echo "===== BLS ENTRIES ====="
sudo ls -l /boot/loader/entries/

echo
echo "===== CURRENT KERNEL CMDLINE ====="
cat /proc/cmdline
```

```ini
===== GRUB CONFIGURATION =====
6:GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX="resume=/dev/mapper/ol-swap rd.lvm.lv=ol/root rd.lvm.lv=ol/swap"
8:GRUB_ENABLE_BLSCFG=true

===== BLS ENTRIES =====
total 12
-rw-r--r--. 1 root root 441 Oct 15 20:53 b1d440aeaf1147159b6893abe25c450a-0-rescue.conf
-rw-r--r--. 1 root root 350 Oct 15 20:51 b1d440aeaf1147159b6893abe25c450a-5.14.0-687.5.3.el9_8.x86_64.conf
-rw-r--r--. 1 root root 401 Oct 15 20:51 b1d440aeaf1147159b6893abe25c450a-6.12.0-203.76.7.3.el9uek.x86_64.conf

===== CURRENT KERNEL CMDLINE =====
BOOT_IMAGE=(hd0,gpt2)/vmlinuz-6.12.0-203.76.7.3.el9uek.x86_64 root=/dev/mapper/ol-root ro resume=/dev/mapper/ol-swap rd.lvm.lv=ol/root rd.lvm.lv=ol/swap
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$

```

Boot configuration is suitable for the standard Oracle Linux 9 procedure: BLS is enabled, and the kernel command line does not currently include a THP parameter.
Oracle's official guidance says to set transparent_hugepage=madvise for UEK 7 and later, regenerate GRUB configuration using --update-bls-cmdline on OL9, and reboot.

# Configure THP persistently
First, back up the GRUB defaults file:
```bash
sudo cp -a /etc/default/grub  /etc/default/grub.pre-oracle19c
sudo vi /etc/default/grub

Find the existing line:
GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX="resume=/dev/mapper/ol-swap rd.lvm.lv=ol/root rd.lvm.lv=ol/swap"

Change it to:
GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX="resume=/dev/mapper/ol-swap rd.lvm.lv=ol/root rd.lvm.lv=ol/swap transparent_hugepage=madvise"

grep '^GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX=' /etc/default/grub
```
### Apply the boot configuration
```bash
sudo grubby --update-kernel=ALL --args="transparent_hugepage=madvise"
sudo grubby --info=ALL | grep -E '^(kernel|args)='
```

### Finish the pre-reboot checks

```bash
echo "===== SYSCTL CONFIGURATION ====="

echo "===== RESOURCE LIMITS ====="

echo
echo "===== ROOT FILESYSTEM SPACE ====="
df -h / /boot /u01

echo
echo "===== ORACLE HOME OWNERSHIP ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
```

# Create our silent-install response file

We'll create a separate working copy named db_install_lab.rsp. This preserves Oracle's original template and lets us configure a software-only installation using your existing svanapal account.

Run this command as svanapal:

```bash
cp "$ORACLE_HOME/install/response/db_install.rsp"  "$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

chmod 600 "$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

ls -l "$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"
```

# Configure the silent-install response file

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1

RSP="$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

sed -i \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.option=.*|oracle.install.option=INSTALL_DB_SWONLY|' \
  -e 's|^UNIX_GROUP_NAME=.*|UNIX_GROUP_NAME=oinstall|' \
  -e 's|^INVENTORY_LOCATION=.*|INVENTORY_LOCATION=/u01/app/oraInventory|' \
  -e "s|^ORACLE_HOME=.*|ORACLE_HOME=$ORACLE_HOME|" \
  -e 's|^ORACLE_BASE=.*|ORACLE_BASE=/u01/app/oracle|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.InstallEdition=.*|oracle.install.db.InstallEdition=EE|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.OSDBA_GROUP=.*|oracle.install.db.OSDBA_GROUP=dba|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.OSOPER_GROUP=.*|oracle.install.db.OSOPER_GROUP=dba|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.OSBACKUPDBA_GROUP=.*|oracle.install.db.OSBACKUPDBA_GROUP=dba|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.OSDGDBA_GROUP=.*|oracle.install.db.OSDGDBA_GROUP=dba|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.OSKMDBA_GROUP=.*|oracle.install.db.OSKMDBA_GROUP=dba|' \
  -e 's|^oracle.install.db.OSRACDBA_GROUP=.*|oracle.install.db.OSRACDBA_GROUP=dba|' \
  "$RSP"
```

```bash
  | cut -d= -f1 | sort | uniq -c
```

# Read the RU 19.26 instructions
Run this command as svanapal to extract the relevant instructions directly to the terminal without modifying any files:
```bash
PATCH_DIR="/media/sf_Sunil-Tech-HyperLab/ISO/Oracle-Softwares/Oracle_Patches/19300_Patches/DB_Patch"

  s/<[^>]+>/ /g;
  s/&nbsp;/ /gi;
  s/&amp;/\&/gi;
  s/&lt;/</gi;
  s/&gt;/>/gi;
  s/&quot;/"/gi;
' |
tr -s '[:space:]' ' ' |
sed -n '/2 Prerequisites/,/3 Installation/p' |
fold -sw 120 |
head -80
```

## Prerequisite Findings — Oracle Database 19c RU 19.26

### 1. Prerequisite Assessment

The following table compares the requirements identified in the RU 19.26 patch documentation with the current lab environment.

| Requirement | RU 19.26 Documentation | Our Lab | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Existing Oracle Database Home | Oracle Database 19c must be installed | Oracle Database 19c software binaries extracted | Pending installation |
| OPatch version | `12.2.0.1.44` or later | `12.2.0.1.45` staged | Version requirement appears satisfied; verify the active OPatch version |
| Required OS tools | `make`, `ar`, `ld`, and `nm` must be available in `PATH` | Not yet verified | Pending |
| Grid Infrastructure Home | This RU must not be installed into a Grid Infrastructure Home | Database Home-only installation planned | Consistent with the plan |
| Backup | Oracle recommends backing up Oracle Home and Central Inventory | Not yet performed | Pending |

# Create the Oracle Home backup
Run this command as svanapal. It creates a compressed archive on your shared drive and leaves the original Oracle Home untouched.

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1

BACKUP_DIR="/media/sf_Sunil-Tech-HyperLab/ISO/Oracle-Softwares/Oracle_Patches/19300_Patches/DB_Software_Backup"
BACKUP_FILE="$BACKUP_DIR/oracle19c_home_pre_opatch_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).tar.gz"

echo "Backup file: $BACKUP_FILE"

tar -czf "$BACKUP_FILE" \
    -C "$(dirname "$ORACLE_HOME")" \
    "$(basename "$ORACLE_HOME")"

if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
    echo "===== BACKUP CREATED ====="
    ls -lh "$BACKUP_FILE"
    echo
    echo "===== ARCHIVE VALIDATION ====="
    gzip -t "$BACKUP_FILE" && echo "Archive integrity check passed"
else
    echo "BACKUP FAILED — do not proceed with OPatch replacement"
fi
```

# Check the inventory pointer
Before updating OPatch, let's check whether the inventory pointer exists elsewhere or whether we simply need to create it for our planned installation.
Run this command as svanapal:

```bash
echo "===== INVENTORY POINTER ====="
if [ -f /etc/oraInst.loc ]; then
    cat /etc/oraInst.loc
else
    echo "/etc/oraInst.loc does not exist"
fi

echo
echo "===== INVENTORY DIRECTORY ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oraInventory

echo
echo "===== INVENTORY CONTENTS ====="
find /u01/app/oraInventory -maxdepth 2 -printf '%M %u:%g %p\n'
```

```ini
===== INVENTORY POINTER =====
/etc/oraInst.loc does not exist

===== INVENTORY DIRECTORY =====
drwxrwxr-x. 2 svanapal oinstall 6 Oct 16 11:03 /u01/app/oraInventory

===== INVENTORY CONTENTS =====
drwxrwxr-x svanapal:oinstall /u01/app/oraInventory
```
Perfect. We have confirmed the cause of the OPatch inventory error.
- /etc/oraInst.loc does not exist.
- /u01/app/oraInventory exists and is owned by svanapal:oinstall.
- The inventory directory is empty because we have not run runInstaller.

# Validate the staged patch directory

```bash

RSP="$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

echo "===== PATCH METADATA ====="
ls -l "$RU_STAGE/etc/config/inventory.xml" \
      "$RU_STAGE/etc/config/actions.xml"

echo
echo "===== PATCH README VERSION ====="
grep -oE '19\.26\.0\.0\.250121|37260974' "$RU_STAGE/README.html" \
  | sort -u

echo
echo "===== RESPONSE FILE KEY SETTINGS ====="
grep -E '^(oracle.install.option|UNIX_GROUP_NAME|INVENTORY_LOCATION|ORACLE_HOME|ORACLE_BASE|oracle.install.db.InstallEdition|oracle.install.db.OSDBA_GROUP)=' "$RSP"

echo
echo "===== OPATCH VERSION ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/OPatch/opatch" version
```

# Final pre-installation check
Before we run runInstaller -applyRU, we need to confirm one important detail: the Oracle 19.3 installer must be able to read the staged RU files and access the response file. We should also ensure the installer environment uses the intended Oracle Home.
Run this as svanapal:

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
RU_STAGE="$HOME/patch_stage/37260974"
RSP="$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

echo "===== INSTALLER ====="
test -x "$ORACLE_HOME/runInstaller" \
  && echo "runInstaller is executable" \
  || echo "ERROR: runInstaller is not executable"

echo
echo "===== RESPONSE FILE ====="
test -r "$RSP" && echo "Response file is readable"

echo
echo "===== RU DIRECTORY ====="
test -r "$RU_STAGE/etc/config/inventory.xml" \
  && echo "RU metadata is readable"

echo
echo "===== STORAGE ====="
df -h /u01 "$HOME"

echo
echo "===== OPATCH ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/OPatch/opatch" version
```

```ini
===== INSTALLER =====
runInstaller is executable

===== RESPONSE FILE =====
Response file is readable

===== RU DIRECTORY =====
RU metadata is readable

===== STORAGE =====
Filesystem           Size  Used Avail Use% Mounted on
/dev/mapper/ol-u01    20G  7.0G   13G  35% /u01
/dev/mapper/ol-root   20G  8.7G   12G  44% /

===== OPATCH =====
OPatch Version: 12.2.0.1.45

OPatch succeeded.
[svanapal@orion9 ~]$
```

# All final pre-installation checks passed.
- runInstaller is executable.
- The response file is readable.
- RU 19.26 metadata is accessible.
- OPatch 12.2.0.1.45 is active.
- /u01 has 13 GB free, and / has 12 GB free.

# Confirm installer options and final response-file settings
Before starting the installation, let's verify the exact command-line options supported by this extracted installer and inspect the remaining settings.
Run as svanapal:

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
RSP="$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

echo "===== INSTALLER OPTIONS ====="
cd "$ORACLE_HOME"
./runInstaller -help 2>&1 |
  grep -iE -A2 -B2 'applyRU|responseFile|silent|waitforcompletion|ignorePrereq'

echo
echo "===== SECURITY UPDATE SETTINGS ====="
grep -niE 'SECURITY_UPDATES_VIA_MYORACLESUPPORT|DECLINE_SECURITY_UPDATES' "$RSP"

echo
echo "===== ROOT SCRIPT SETTING ====="
grep -n '^oracle.install.db.rootconfig.executeRootScript=' "$RSP"
```
```ini
===== INSTALLER OPTIONS =====
Following are the possible flags:
        -help - display help.
        -silent - run in silent mode. The inputs can be a response file or a list of command line variable value pairs.
                [-ignorePrereqFailure - ignore all prerequisite checks failures.]
        -responseFile - specify the complete path of the response file to use.
        -logLevel - enable the log of messages up to the priority level provided in this argument. Valid options are: severe, warning, info, config, fine, finer, finest.
        -executePrereqs | -executeConfigTools | -createGoldImage
--
        -printmemory - log the debug information for the memory usage.
        -printtime - log the debug information for the time usage.
        -waitForCompletion - wait for the completion of the installation, instead of spawning the installer and returning the console prompt.
        -noconfig - do not execute the config tools.
        -noconsole - suppress the display of messages in the console. The console is not allocated.
        -ignoreInternalDriverError - ignore any internal driver errors.
        -noCopy - perform the configuration without copying the software on to the remote nodes.
        -applyRU - apply release update to the Oracle home.
        -applyOneOffs - apply one-off patch to the Oracle home. Multiple one-off patches can be passed as a comma separated list of locations.


===== SECURITY UPDATE SETTINGS =====

===== ROOT SCRIPT SETTING =====
120:oracle.install.db.rootconfig.executeRootScript=
```

Good. Your output confirms that the Oracle 19c installer supports both -applyRU and -executePrereqs.

# Run the installer prerequisite check

This is the closest built-in validation step available in the installer help you displayed. It checks installation prerequisites; it is not a complete simulation of applying RU 19.26.
Run as svanapal:

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
RSP="$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"

cd "$ORACLE_HOME"

./runInstaller -silent \
  -executePrereqs \
  -responseFile "$RSP"
```
```ini
Launching Oracle Database Setup Wizard...
```

Good. The prerequisite-check command started the Oracle Database Setup Wizard, but the output alone does not confirm whether the checks passed or failed.

# Check the prerequisite-check logs
Run this command as svanapal:

```bash
echo "===== INSTALLER PROCESS ====="
ps -ef | grep -E '[r]unInstaller|[o]raInstall'

echo
echo "===== RECENT INSTALLER LOG DIRECTORIES ====="
find /tmp -maxdepth 1 -type d -name 'OraInstall*' \
  -printf '%TY-%Tm-%Td %TH:%TM %p\n' | sort -r | head -5

echo
echo "===== INVENTORY LOG DIRECTORY ====="
ls -ld /u01/app/oraInventory/logs 2>/dev/null \
  || echo "Inventory logs directory not created"
```
```ini
===== INSTALLER PROCESS =====

===== RECENT INSTALLER LOG DIRECTORIES =====

===== INVENTORY LOG DIRECTORY =====
Inventory logs directory not created
[svanapal@orion9 dbhome_1]$
```
The prerequisite-check process is no longer running, and no installer log directory was found under /tmp. The central inventory logs directory also hasn't been created.
This means we don't yet have evidence that the prerequisite checks completed successfully. It doesn't necessarily mean they failed.

# Locate the installer logs
Let's search the Oracle Home and your home directory for recent installer logs before retrying anything.
Run as svanapal:

```bash
echo "===== RECENT ORACLE INSTALLER LOGS ====="
find "$HOME" /u01/app/oracle -type f \
  \( -name 'installActions*.log' -o -name 'oraInstall*.out' -o -name 'oraInstall*.err' \) \
  -mmin -30 -printf '%TY-%Tm-%Td %TH:%TM %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -r | head -20

echo
echo "===== RECENT TEMPORARY INSTALLER FILES ====="
find /tmp /var/tmp -maxdepth 3 -type f \
  \( -name 'installActions*.log' -o -name 'oraInstall*.out' -o -name 'oraInstall*.err' \) \
  -mmin -30 -printf '%TY-%Tm-%Td %TH:%TM %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -r | head -20

echo
echo "===== ORACLE INVENTORY POINTER ====="
ls -l /etc/oraInst.loc 2>/dev/null || echo "oraInst.loc still absent"
```
```ini

2026-10-16 14:56 /tmp/InstallActions2026-10-16_02-56-08PM/installActions2026-10-16_02-56-08PM.log

===== ORACLE INVENTORY POINTER =====
oraInst.loc still absent
```
Good — we found the prerequisite-check log. The installer wrote its log under /tmp, even though the command returned to the shell.

We have now identified the actual problem. The prerequisite check did not complete successfully. Oracle's Cluster Verification Utility (CVU) encountered an exception while trying to initialize its platform-specific storage checks.

The key lines in your log are:
==== Distribution Id determined to be null
Value not configured for CV_ASSUME_DISTID
Exception in thread "main"
java.lang.ExceptionInInitializerError
Caused by: java.lang.NullPointerException
at oracle.ops.verification.framework.storage.StorageUtil.<clinit>

The installer detected Oracle Linux 9.8, but CVU could not determine a distribution ID from its built-in configuration. This caused the prerequisite-check workflow to terminate. It does not establish that the operating system itself or your response file is invalid.
For this Oracle 19.3 installer running on Oracle Linux 9.8, we should investigate the distribution-ID handling before moving forward.


# Check Oracle's CVU configuration
Run this as svanapal. 

This is a read-only inspection; it does not change your installation.

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1

echo "===== CVU CONFIG FILES ====="
find "$ORACLE_HOME/cv" -maxdepth 3 -type f \
  \( -name 'cvu_config' -o -name 'cvu_prereq.xml' -o -name 'variables.xml' \) \
  -print

echo
echo "===== CVU CONFIGURATION ====="
if [ -f "$ORACLE_HOME/cv/admin/cvu_config" ]; then
    cat "$ORACLE_HOME/cv/admin/cvu_config"
else
    echo "No cvu_config at the standard location."
fi

echo
echo "===== DISTRIBUTION-ID REFERENCES ====="
grep -RinE 'CV_ASSUME_DISTID|OL9|OEL9|RHEL9|9\.8' \
  "$ORACLE_HOME/cv/admin" "$ORACLE_HOME/cv/cvdata" 2>/dev/null | head -80
```

```ini
CV_RAW_CHECK_ENABLED=TRUE

# Fallback to this distribution id
#CV_ASSUME_DISTID=OEL5

#Complete file system path of sudo binary file, default is /usr/local/bin/sudo
CV_SUDO_BINARY_LOCATION=/usr/local/bin/sudo

#Complete file system path of pbrun binary file, default is /usr/local/bin/pbrun
CV_PBRUN_BINARY_LOCATION=/usr/local/bin/pbrun

# Whether X-Windows check should be performed for user equivalence with SSH
#CV_XCHK_FOR_SSH_ENABLED=TRUE

# To override SSH location
#ORACLE_SRVM_REMOTESHELL=/usr/bin/ssh

# To override SCP location
#ORACLE_SRVM_REMOTECOPY=/usr/bin/scp

# To override version used by command line parser
CV_ASSUME_CL_VERSION=19.1.0.0.0

# Location of the browser to be used to display HTML report
#CV_DEFAULT_BROWSER_LOCATION=/usr/bin/mozilla

# Maximum number of retries for discover DHCP server
#CV_MAX_RETRIES_DHCP_DISCOVERY=5

# Maximum CVU trace files size (in multiples of 100 MB)
#CV_TRACE_SIZE_MULTIPLIER=1

/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1/cv/cvdata/ora_software_cfg.xml:47181:         <File Path="md/property_graph/lib/" Name="jackson-databind-2.9.8.jar"/>
```

Excellent. Your output confirms that the Oracle 19.3 home contains the expected CVU configuration file:
/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1/cv/admin/cvu_config

The important setting is currently commented out:
#CV_ASSUME_DISTID=OEL5

The installer log showed that it detected Oracle Linux 9.8 but could not determine a distribution ID. That is consistent with the CVU initialization failure we observed.
We should not uncomment OEL5. That would tell the old installer to assume an outdated operating-system identity, which is not an appropriate assumption for Oracle Linux 9.8.

Run:
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export CV_ASSUME_DISTID=OL7

echo "CV_ASSUME_DISTID=$CV_ASSUME_DISTID"


Expected output:
CV_ASSUME_DISTID=OL7

This sets a temporary environment variable for the current terminal session only; it does not edit cvu_config or permanently change your system. Oracle documents this compatibility setting in its Linux 9 installation guidance for 19c components.

# Re-run the prerequisite check
We will repeat the same check with CV_ASSUME_DISTID=OL7 set. This will tell us whether the distribution-ID error is resolved; it does not guarantee that all prerequisites will pass.
Run this as svanapal:

```bash
cd "$ORACLE_HOME"

./runInstaller -silent \
  -executePrereqs \
  -responseFile "$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp"
```

```bash
LOGDIR="/u01/app/oraInventory/logs/InstallActions2026-10-16_07-23-46PM"

echo
echo "===== LOG FILES ====="
find "$LOGDIR" -maxdepth 2 -type f -printf '%p\n' | sort
```

## Oracle Database 19c — Prerequisite Findings on `orion9`

### 1. Prerequisite Check Results

The Oracle Database 19c installer reported the following prerequisite findings on the `orion9` server.

| Check | Result | What It Means |
|---|---|---|
| Swap space | Failed — 2 GB found; approximately 7.73 GB required | The VM has less swap space than the installer expects. |
| `compat-libcap1` | Missing | The compatibility package expected by the Oracle Database 19.3 prerequisite rules is absent. |
| `net-tools` | Missing | The legacy networking utilities package is absent. |

### 2. Installer Result

| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| Installer warning code | `INS-13014` |
| Installer status | `SUCCESS_WITH_WARNINGS` |
| Prerequisite classification | All three findings reported as `IGNORABLE` |
| Overall assessment | Installation proceeded with warnings rather than a clean prerequisite pass |

**Important:** An `IGNORABLE` classification means the installer permits the finding to be acknowledged or bypassed under its prerequisite rules. It does not mean that the underlying requirement has been satisfied.

# Check the current swap and available disk space

```bash
echo "===== MEMORY AND SWAP ====="
free -h
swapon --show

echo
echo "===== FILESYSTEM CAPACITY ====="
df -h / /u01 /tmp

echo
echo "===== EXISTING SWAP CONFIGURATION ====="
grep -vE '^[[:space:]]*(#|$)' /etc/fstab
```
## Oracle Database 19c — Memory, Swap, and Filesystem Assessment

**Server:** `orion9`

### 1. Memory and Swap Utilization

The following results were collected from the server.

| Resource | Total | Used | Free / Available |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Physical memory | 7.7 GiB | 609 MiB | 7.1 GiB available |
| Swap space | 2.0 GiB | 0 B | 2.0 GiB |

**Active swap device:**

| Device | Type | Size | Used | Priority |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `/dev/dm-1` | Partition | 2 GiB | 0 B | `-2` |

**Assessment:** The VM currently has 2 GiB of swap space, with no swap in use. The Oracle Database 19c installer previously reported an approximate swap requirement of 7.73 GB.

### 2. Filesystem Capacity

| Filesystem | Size | Used | Available | Utilization | Mount Point |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `/dev/mapper/ol-root` | 20 GB | 8.7 GB | 12 GB | 44% | `/` |
| `/dev/mapper/ol-u01` | 20 GB | 7.0 GB | 13 GB | 35% | `/u01` |
| `/dev/mapper/ol-root` | 20 GB | 8.7 GB | 12 GB | 44% | `/` |

**Assessment:**

- The root filesystem has approximately 12 GB available.
- The `/u01` filesystem has approximately 13 GB available.
- The root filesystem appears twice in the collected output; it is the same filesystem, not an additional storage allocation.

### 3. Existing Swap Configuration

The `/etc/fstab` output contains the following swap entry:

```fstab
/dev/mapper/ol-swap none swap defaults 0 0
```

The VM therefore has a configured swap logical volume at `/dev/mapper/ol-swap`.

Other relevant filesystem entries include:

```fstab
/dev/mapper/ol-root /     xfs  defaults 0 0
/dev/mapper/ol-u01  /u01  xfs  defaults 0 0
/dev/mapper/ol-u02  /u02  xfs  defaults 0 0
/dev/mapper/ol-u03  /u03  xfs  defaults 0 0
/dev/mapper/ol-u04  /u04  xfs  defaults 0 0
```

### 4. Findings and Recommended Action

| Check | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Physical memory | 7.7 GiB total | Recorded |
| Active swap | 2 GiB | Below the installer-reported requirement |
| Swap utilization | 0 B used | Recorded |
| Root filesystem | 12 GB available | Recorded |

# Confirm the existing swap logical volume

swap is already configured on /dev/mapper/ol-swap, so we should inspect the existing LVM volume before creating a swap file.

Run the following commands as root:
```bash
echo "===== SWAP LOGICAL VOLUME ====="
lvs -o lv_name,vg_name,lv_size,lv_path

echo
echo "===== VOLUME GROUP FREE SPACE ====="
vgs -o vg_name,vg_size,vg_free

echo
echo "===== PHYSICAL VOLUMES ====="
pvs -o pv_name,vg_name,pv_size,pv_free
```

```ini
===== SWAP LOGICAL VOLUME =====
  LV   VG LSize  Path
  root ol 20.00g /dev/ol/root
  swap ol  2.00g /dev/ol/swap
  u01  ol 20.00g /dev/ol/u01
  u02  ol 10.00g /dev/ol/u02
  u03  ol 10.00g /dev/ol/u03
  u04  ol  5.00g /dev/ol/u04

===== VOLUME GROUP FREE SPACE =====
  VG VSize  VFree
  ol 67.00g 4.00m

===== PHYSICAL VOLUMES =====
  PV         VG PSize  PFree
  /dev/sda3  ol 67.00g 4.00m
```

Excellent. The output confirms that your Oracle Linux VM uses LVM, but the volume group ol has virtually no unallocated space.

# Choose the safest swap solution
We need approximately 8 GiB total swap to meet the Oracle prerequisite check, which currently reports a requirement of about 7.73 GiB.
Because the LVM volume group has no free space, I recommend creating a 6 GiB swap file on / rather than resizing your existing logical volumes. Your root filesystem currently has 12 GiB free, so this should fit without changing your LVM layout.

Before making the change
Run this single read-only command as root to confirm that the filesystem supports the proposed swap file:

```bash
findmnt -no FSTYPE /
```
```ini
[root@orion9 ~]# findmnt -no FSTYPE /
xfs
```

# Create and activate the additional swap file
This step creates a 6 GiB file, secures it, formats it as swap, and activates it for the current boot.
Run the following commands as root.

```bash
echo "===== CREATE 6 GiB SWAP FILE ====="
dd if=/dev/zero of=/swapfile bs=1M count=6144 status=progress

echo "===== SET SECURE PERMISSIONS ====="
chmod 600 /swapfile

echo "===== FORMAT AS SWAP ====="
mkswap /swapfile

echo "===== ACTIVATE SWAP FILE ====="
swapon /swapfile
```
```ini
===== CREATE 6 GiB SWAP FILE =====
5575278592 bytes (5.6 GB, 5.2 GiB) copied, 4 s, 1.4 GB/s
6144+0 records in
6144+0 records out
6442450944 bytes (6.4 GB, 6.0 GiB) copied, 4.72549 s, 1.4 GB/s
===== SET SECURE PERMISSIONS =====
===== FORMAT AS SWAP =====
Setting up swapspace version 1, size = 6 GiB (6442446848 bytes)
no label, UUID=7a722f5c-f1c0-4287-8a6e-8a31465d7bf4
===== ACTIVATE SWAP FILE =====
```

Before making any persistent configuration changes, run these commands as root:

```bash

echo

echo
echo "===== ROOT FILESYSTEM SPACE ====="
```

Excellent! Your Oracle Linux VM now has 8 GiB of active swap, meeting the Oracle 19c prerequisite check's reported requirement of approximately 7.73 GiB.

Make the swap file persistent
The swap file is active now, but it won't automatically activate after a reboot unless we add it to /etc/fstab.
As root, first check whether /swapfile is already configured:

```bash
grep -nE '^[[:space:]]*/swapfile[[:space:]]' /etc/fstab
```

```ini
[root@orion9 ~]# grep -nE '^[[:space:]]*/swapfile[[:space:]]' /etc/fstab
[root@orion9 ~]#
```

# Persist the swap file
Run these commands as root:
```bash
echo '/swapfile none swap defaults 0 0' >> /etc/fstab

Verify the entry:
grep -nE '^[[:space:]]*/swapfile[[:space:]]' /etc/fstab
```

```ini
[root@orion9 ~]# grep -nE '^[[:space:]]*/swapfile[[:space:]]' /etc/fstab
20:/swapfile none swap defaults 0 0
```



There are two warnings to understand:
- target specified more than once: both the existing LVM swap and /swapfile use the target none. This is normal for multiple swap entries and is not, by itself, a problem.
- non-bind mount source /swapfile is a directory or regular file: findmnt --verify does not fully validate swap-file entries as ordinary filesystem mounts.
  Since swapon --show confirms /swapfile is active, this warning alone does not indicate a failure.

# Start the Oracle 19c software installation with RU 19.26

Run the following as svanapalt. This command installs database software only; it does not create a database or configure a listener.

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export CV_ASSUME_DISTID=OL7

cd "$ORACLE_HOME"

./runInstaller \
  -silent \
  -applyRU /u02/patch_stage/37260974 \
  -responseFile "$HOME/db_install_lab.rsp" \
  -waitforcompletion
```

# Verify the Oracle executable version
```bash
su - svanapal

Then run:
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

echo "===== SQL*Plus VERSION ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/sqlplus" -V

echo
echo "===== INSTALLED PATCHES ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/OPatch/opatch" lspatches
```

```ini
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"
```

# Verify the Oracle Home binaries
Run this as svanapal. We will check the Oracle executable and the inventory details before proceeding to database creation.

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export ORACLE_BASE=/u01/app/oracle
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

echo "===== ORACLE HOME ====="
echo "$ORACLE_HOME"

echo
echo "===== ORACLE EXECUTABLE ====="
ls -lh "$ORACLE_HOME/bin/oracle"

echo
echo "===== ORACLE HOME SPACE ====="
df -h "$ORACLE_HOME"

echo
echo "===== ORACLE HOME INVENTORY ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/OPatch/opatch" lsinventory -oh "$ORACLE_HOME" | tail -20
```

# Check filesystem capacity

```bash
echo "===== FILESYSTEM CAPACITY ====="
df -h / /u01 /u02 /u03 /u04

echo
echo "===== ORACLE DIRECTORY SIZES ====="
du -sh /u01/app/oracle /u01/app/oraInventory 2>/dev/null
```

# Check the Oracle Database Configuration Assistant
Before creating the database, let's verify that DBCA is present and executable in the patched Oracle Home.
Run this as svanapal:

```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

echo "===== DBCA VERSION ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca" -version

echo
echo "===== DBCA EXECUTABLE ====="
ls -lh "$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca"
```

```ini
[svanapal@orion9 dbhome_1]$ export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

echo "===== DBCA VERSION ====="
"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca" -version

echo
echo "===== DBCA EXECUTABLE ====="
ls -lh "$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca"
===== DBCA VERSION =====
DISPLAY not set.
Set DISPLAY environment variable, then re-run.

===== DBCA EXECUTABLE =====
-rwxr-x---. 1 svanapal oinstall 7.6K Oct 16 20:39 /u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1/bin/dbca
```

# Verify DBCA silent-mode support
Run this as svanapal:
```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca" -help
```

```ini
[svanapal@orion9 dbhome_1]$ export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca" -help

Usage:  dbca [<flag>] [<command> <option>]
Following are the possible flags:
        -help
                -contextId <RAC | SI>
        -ignorePreReqs - Ignore prerequisite checks for current operation.
        -ignorePrereqFailure  - Ignore all prerequisites failures.
        -silent - This flag allows you to carry on configuration in silent mode.
                -<command>

Following are the possible commands:
        -addInstance - Command to Add an instance to an admin managed cluster database.
        -configureDatabase - Command to Configure a database.
        -configurePluggableDatabase - Command to Configure a pluggable database.
        -createCloneTemplate - Command to Create a clone template from an existing database.
        -createDatabase - Command to Create a database.
        -createDuplicateDB - Command to Duplicate a database.
        -createPluggableDatabase - Command to Create a pluggable database.
        -createTemplateFromDB - Command to Create a template from an existing database.
        -createTemplateFromTemplate - Command to Create a template from an existing template.
        -deleteDatabase - Command to Delete a database.
        -deleteInstance - Command to Delete an instance from an admin managed cluster database.
        -deletePluggableDatabase - Command to Delete a pluggable database.
        -deleteTemplate - Command to delete a template.
        -executePrereqs - Command to execute prerequisite checks.
        -generateScripts - Command to Generate scripts which can be used to create database.
        -relocatePDB - Command to Relocate a pluggable database.
        -unplugDatabase - Command to Unplug a pluggable database.


[svanapal@orion9 dbhome_1]$
```

# Check DBCA database-creation prerequisites
Run this as svanapal:
```bash
export ORACLE_HOME=/u01/app/oracle/product/19.0.0/dbhome_1
export ORACLE_BASE=/u01/app/oracle
export PATH="$ORACLE_HOME/bin:$PATH"

"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca" -silent -executePrereqs -databaseConfigType SINGLE
```

```ini
"$ORACLE_HOME/bin/dbca" -silent -executePrereqs -databaseConfigType SINGLE
[FATAL] [DBT-09101] Target environment does not meet some mandatory requirements.
   CAUSE: Some of the mandatory prerequisites are not met. See logs for details. /u01/app/oracle/cfgtoollogs/dbca/trace.log_2026-10-16_09-14-13PM
   ACTION: Find the appropriate configuration from the log file or from the installation guide to meet the prerequisites and fix this manually.
```

# Identify the failing prerequisite
Run the following commands as svanapal on orion9:
```bash
LOG=/u01/app/oracle/cfgtoollogs/dbca/trace.log_2026-10-16_09-14-13PM

echo "===== DBCA FATAL / ERROR / FAILED MESSAGES ====="
grep -n -i -E 'FATAL|ERROR|FAILED|mandatory|DBT-|PRVF-|PRVG-' "$LOG" | tail -80

echo
echo "===== END OF DBCA TRACE LOG ====="
tail -100 "$LOG"
```
